Theorem 217.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any neuroscience module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 651. The projective resolution has length at most 951 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by neuroscience generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 651, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 951. QED.

Theorem 217.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on neuroscience structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 254. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 283 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the neuroscience decomposition. By minimality, the element a^254 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 283-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
